CHANGELOG.md:9:- **`tools/usage-to-dashboard-ndjson.mjs`: documented dashboard-integration bridge silently dropped every v:1 usage row (#112).** The translator was written for the preload-era `usage.jsonl` schema (`entry.timestamp`, `entry.q5h_pct` / `entry.q7d_pct` as int 0-100). The proxy `usage-log` extension introduced in v3.2.0 writes MeterRowSchema v:1 with three field renames (`entry.ts`, `entry.q5h` / `entry.q7d` as float 0-1). The translator's entry guard `if (!entry.timestamp) return null` silently dropped every v:1 row, so external dashboards consuming the bridge received zero data from proxy-mode sessions. The integration is documented in our README's Companion Tools section and `docs/dashboard-integration.md` — this was a documented-feature regression. Fix: entry guard, quota-header reconstruction, `ts_start`/`ts_end` mapping, and `req_id` generation now accept both schemas via fallback (preload-era field if present, else v:1 field). Backwards-compatible — both formats work, no migration required. Adds 16 regression tests covering both schemas plus parity (`req_id` is identical for the same logical request expressed in either schema, so dashboards that dedup on `req_id` won't see duplicates from a user upgrading preload→proxy). Reported by [@TomTheMenace](https://github.com/TomTheMenace) with a tested patch already in the issue body — thank you.
CHANGELOG.md:19:- **`tools/quota-statusline.sh`: shell injection via Python triple-quoted literal (#108).** The v3.5.0 statusline rewrite interpolated CC's hook stdin payload directly into a Python triple-quoted string (`json.loads('''$input''')`). A `'''` byte sequence anywhere in the payload closed the literal early and let the following bytes execute as Python in the user's CC process. Because CC's hook payload reflects user-controlled paths (`cwd`, `workspace.current_dir`, `workspace.project_dir`, `transcript_path`) and apostrophes are legal in filesystem paths, a hostile directory name on disk (planted via `git clone`, archive extraction, npm package, etc.) could trigger arbitrary local code execution at the user's privilege every time CC redrew the statusline. **Severity: local code execution, persistent re-fire on every statusline tick, no user interaction beyond `cd`-ing into the hostile path.** Fix: capture stdin in bash, `export CC_INPUT`, and pipe the Python source through a single-quoted heredoc (`<<'PYEOF'`) which disables ALL bash interpolation in the body. Python now reads the JSON via `os.environ.get('CC_INPUT')`, where the bytes are inert at every layer. Adds T6 + T7 regression tests that drive the exact `'''+__import__('os').system(...)+'''` pattern against the script under a tmpdir-rooted `HOME` and assert the sentinel file is never created. Reported by [@schuay (Jakob Linke)](https://github.com/schuay) in [#108](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/issues/108) — thank you for the responsible disclosure.
CHANGELOG.md:29:- `cache-telemetry`: session-id headers (`x-claude-code-session-id` and the legacy fallbacks) live on the **request**, not the response. The v3.5.0 implementation read them from `ctx.headers` in `onResponseStart` — but that ctx carries response headers, and Anthropic doesn't echo session-id back. Net effect on multi-agent hosts running v3.5.0: every per-session file landed at `sessions/unknown.json` with `session_id: null`, defeating the whole point of the per-session split. Captured production failure on visits-01 immediately after v3.5.0 rollout. Fix moves session-id resolution into a new `onRequest` hook (request headers); `onStreamEvent` reads from `ctx.meta._sessionId` as before. The proxy server passes the same `meta` object through `onRequest → onResponseStart → onStreamEvent`, so the threading works end-to-end. Adds two regression tests that drive request and response headers separately to prevent recurrence.
CHANGELOG.md:47:698 → 733 (+35): new tests for the `sessionFilename` rule, file-write happy paths and fallbacks, atomic write contract, legacy-file cleanup (one-shot per process), TTL sweep behavior + throttling + env-override, microcompact session-id fallback chain precedence, and pipeline integration covering happy path, two-session interleaving, and path-traversal safety. Plus T1–T5 statusline smoke tests covering UUID happy path, missing session_id (file present and absent), warming-state, all-files-missing clean exit, and malformed session_id reading the hashed filename.
CHANGELOG.md:53:- New extension `messages-cache-breakpoint` (order 410, opt-in via `CACHE_FIX_INJECT_MESSAGES_BREAKPOINT=1`) — injects the missing breakpoint #3 `cache_control` marker at the boundary between Claude Code's auto-injected `messages[0]` blocks (hooks, skills, project CLAUDE.md, deferred-tools, MCP server descriptions) and the first real user content. Anthropic's prompt cache supports up to 4 markers per request; CC currently uses 3, leaving the auto-injected span uncached. Conservative: skips on 0 markers (non-CC baseline) and refuses at 4 markers (would 400 the request). Five-kind boundary detection with fail-open classification. Adds `CACHE_FIX_DUMP_MESSAGES_HEAD=<path>` diagnostic dump for fixture sourcing. (#90, closes #12; @wadabum's 4-breakpoint analysis at anthropics/claude-code#47098)
CHANGELOG.md:55:- New extension `ttl-tier-detect` (order 75, default-enabled, no env var required) — detects `cache_control.ttl="5m"` markers in the incoming payload before downstream extensions strip them, recording the result on `ctx.meta._ttlTier`. Pure detection, no mutation. Ports the in-payload tier-detection from `preload.mjs:1815-1828`. (#100, closes #97; @vmfarms surfaced this)
CHANGELOG.md:59:- `ttl-management` now consumes `ctx.meta._ttlTier` and auto-upgrades injected TTL: when the incoming payload carries any `ttl="5m"` marker, all injected `cache_control` blocks get `ttl="5m"`, even if `CACHE_FIX_TTL_MAIN` / `CACHE_FIX_TTL_SUBAGENT` is set to `1h`. Env value `none` still suppresses injection entirely. (#100)
CHANGELOG.md:64:- `image-strip`: legacy `[image-strip]` and v3.3.0 `[image-guard]` operational stderr summaries fired on every request that did observable work, regardless of `CACHE_FIX_DEBUG`. CHANGELOG.md:93:Execution order: **Pass 0 → Pass 3 → Pass 1 → Pass 2 → count cap**. Each pass is independent — Pass 1 never resizes; Pass 3 never strips. README's precedence matrix documents every supported env-var combination.
CHANGELOG.md:99:**New env vars:**
CHANGELOG.md:117:New `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_MAX_DIM=<pixels>` env var on the existing `image-strip` extension. When an image's pixel dimensions exceed the cap (Anthropic's per-image dimension ceiling for many-image requests is 2000px), the image is replaced with a forensic placeholder noting the original dimensions and tool_use_id. Covers both user-message direct images and tool_result-nested images. Pure-JS PNG and JPEG header parsing in new `proxy/image-dimensions.mjs` — no native dependencies.
CHANGELOG.md:119:Composes with the existing `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_KEEP_LAST` (count axis): when both are set, `KEEP_LAST` runs first (drops images from old messages), then `MAX_DIM` runs on whatever survives (caps the size of the kept ones). Common triggers for the dimension axis: hi-res manuscript scans, retina screenshots, photos at full resolution.
CHANGELOG.md:137:Read-only structural fingerprinter that detects when CC ships updates that change `/v1/messages` request shape (cache_control marker count, system block layout, tools list, system-reminder patterns, beta headers). Per-namespace baseline persists across proxy restarts at `~/.claude/upstream-baseline.json` (atomic tmp + rename with unique suffix). Events appended to `~/.claude/upstream-changes.jsonl`. **Mechanically content-free**: every persisted field is a count, position, boolean, bucket label, or hash of stable identifiers. Allowlist matches stored as hash-of-sorted-indices, never the matched text. Unknown-marker / unknown-pattern detection records ONLY a boolean. Tested with a "secret string" planted throughout a request body — never appears in the fingerprint.
CHANGELOG.md:143:**Cross-repo release ordering**: cache-fix v3.2.0 ships first. claude-meter v0.4.0 follows, declaring `claude-code-cache-fix >= 3.2.0` as its supported producer. The two packages are NOT independently shippable for the proxy-mode ingestion path.

CHANGELOG.md:187:- **`deferred-tools-restore`** (defaults ON; opt out via `CACHE_FIX_SKIP_DEFERRED_TOOLS_RESTORE=1`) — preserves cache prefix across the MCP-reconnect race. On `claude --continue`, if MCP servers haven't reconnected before the first post-resume request, the deferred-tools attachment block at `msg[0]` shrinks dramatically and busts the cache at the very top (entire ~940K prompt re-caches). This extension persists the clean form of the block and substitutes it on subsequent shrunken requests, with strict downgrade guard (snapshot must be strictly longer than current). Snapshot keyed on the cwd parsed from CC's `# Environment` section in the system prompt — line-based section parser with ambiguity guard fails open on parse failure. Closes #59 item 6. (#66)

CHANGELOG.md:232:- **BUGFIX: TTL ordering violation causes 400 error at Q5h=100%** — When the user's quota hit 100%, CC places `ttl: "5m"` markers. Our interceptor then added `ttl: "1h"` markers on other blocks, violating Anthropic's ordering constraint (1h cannot follow 5m in tools→system→messages order). Fix: detect existing TTL tier from the payload before any extension runs. If any block has `ttl: "5m"`, all injected markers (TTL injection, `cache_control_normalize`, `cache_control_sticky`) now use `5m` to match. Reported by @cowwoc (#44).
CHANGELOG.md:238:- **New tool: `manual-compact.sh`** — Manual compaction for sessions using the 1M context hack (`DISABLE_COMPACT=1`). Supports project directory auto-detection with confirmation prompt, and optional user context file for known gaps. Tested at 95% active-work fidelity. See `tools/MANUAL-COMPACT.md`.
CHANGELOG.md:246:- **BUGFIX: `cache_control_sticky` still exceeded 4-marker limit on CC v2.1.112** — v2.0.2 reduced `MAX_POSITIONS` from 3→2 assuming CC uses exactly 2 markers (1 system + 1 messages). CC v2.1.112 uses 3 markers in some configurations, so 2 sticky + 3 CC = 5, still exceeding the hard limit. Fix: count all existing `cache_control` markers across the full body (system + messages) before adding sticky markers, and cap the total at 4. No more assumptions about CC's marker budget. Caused `400 invalid_request_error` in production.

README.md:56:- **Linux** → `~/.config/systemd/user/cache-fix-proxy.service` (systemd user unit)
README.md:68:**Auto-recovery (Linux):** `install-service` also drops a healthcheck companion (`cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.service` + `.timer`). The timer fires every 2 minutes; the oneshot service runs `curl -fs http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health` and `systemctl --user start cache-fix-proxy.service` if the probe fails. This recovers the proxy from any stop — clean or unclean, expected or unexpected — within 2 minutes. Background: `Restart=on-failure` doesn't fire on clean stops, so before this companion existed, a `systemctl stop` from any source (including unidentified ones during an Anthropic outage on 2026-04-25) would leave the proxy down indefinitely. macOS doesn't need the companion — launchd's `KeepAlive` already auto-restarts on any exit.

README.md:103:Use `--restart=always` instead of the systemd healthcheck companion — Docker handles auto-recovery natively. Mount nothing; the container is stateless. Override the default port with `-e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT=...`. Override the upstream (e.g. to chain through llm-relay) with `-e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM=http://host.docker.internal:8080`. The image runs as the unprivileged `node` user (uid 1000) and exposes a `HEALTHCHECK` Docker can use for liveness.

README.md:117:**Linux note:** the chained-upstream `host.docker.internal` example below is automatic on Docker Desktop (macOS / Windows). On plain Linux Docker Engine you usually need `--add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway` so the name resolves to the host bridge. Without it, the container's name lookup fails and the proxy can't reach the upstream service running on the host. Example chaining cache-fix proxy through `llm-relay` running on the host:

README.md:185:The [VS Code extension](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix-vscode) (v0.5.0) supports both proxy and preload modes:
README.md:190:3. Restart any active Claude Code session
README.md:206:**What it does NOT do:** No network calls from the proxy or interceptor. All telemetry is written to local files under `~/.claude/`. No data leaves your machine.
README.md:228:**Proxy mode** (v3.0.0+): An HTTP server on `localhost:9801` intercepts `POST /v1/messages` requests. Seven extension modules process each request through a pipeline — normalizing block order, stripping fingerprints, stabilizing tool sort, managing TTL markers. Extensions are hot-reloadable `.mjs` files configured in `proxy/extensions.json`. All other traffic passes through untouched.
README.md:230:**Preload mode** (v2.x): A Node.js `--import` module that patches `globalThis.fetch` before Claude Code makes API calls. Applies the same fixes inline — scans user messages for relocated blocks, sorts tools, recomputes fingerprints, injects TTL markers.
README.md:320:export CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_GIT_INSTRUCTIONS=1
README.md:325:**Why we don't ship a proxy extension for this:** the proxy intercepts requests after Claude Code has already composed the system prompt — by then the volatile `git status` text is already part of the prefix that the model conditioned on in the previous turn, and stripping it post-hoc would itself bust the cache. The fix has to happen at the source. `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_GIT_INSTRUCTIONS=1` prevents the injection before the prompt is composed, which is why the native flag is the right tool. Stripping post-hoc would also remove model-visible context that an explicit Bash call can recover, and would risk false-positive matches against assistant-written text.
README.md:345:Your script should try the v3.5.0+ proxy paths first and fall back to the legacy path if not present. That way it works in both modes (and on hosts mid-upgrade). The session id usually comes from Claude Code's stdin when it invokes a statusline hook; for other consumers, capture it from the most-recently-modified `~/.claude/projects/*/*.jsonl` filename.
README.md:399:import { readFileSync, existsSync } from "node:fs";
README.md:400:import { homedir } from "node:os";
README.md:401:import { join } from "node:path";
README.md:402:import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
README.md:439:On multi-agent hosts (multiple Claude Code sessions sharing one proxy), the pre-v3.5.0 single global file caused every session to overwrite the others' cache stats with each response. A statusline reading from session A would show session B's TTL tier whenever B sent a request more recently. Per-session files plus an account-global quota file resolve this without losing the easy account-wide view. See [#104](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/issues/104) for the original report.
README.md:446:export CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_KEEP_LAST=3
README.md:454:export CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_MAX_DIM=2000
README.md:468:The two compose: with both set, `KEEP_LAST` runs first (drops the count), then `MAX_DIM` runs on what remains (caps the size of the kept ones). Common triggers for the dimension axis: hi-res manuscript scans, retina screenshots, photos at full resolution.
README.md:470:Pure-JS PNG and JPEG header parsing — no native deps. Other formats (GIF, WebP, AVIF, BMP) pass through unchanged regardless of dimension. Fail-open: images whose dimensions can't be parsed (truncated header, unsupported format) are kept rather than stripped — better to send a request that might error than to strip a valid image we just couldn't measure.
README.md:474:A conditional pipeline that mirrors Anthropic's actual rules. Strictly opt-in via a single env var:

README.md:528:Anthropic's prompt cache supports up to **four** `cache_control` markers per request. Claude Code currently uses three of the four; the third (between auto-injected `messages[0]` content — hooks, skills, project CLAUDE.md, deferred tools, MCP server descriptions — and the first real user content) is missing entirely. Without that marker, every change inside the auto-injected span busts the cache for everything that follows. wadabum projected ~6,500 token savings per fresh-session first turn from adding it ([anthropics/claude-code#47098](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/47098)).

  README.md:7:Cache optimization proxy for [Claude Code](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code). Fixes prompt 
cache bugs that cause excessive quota burn, stabilizes the request prefix, and monitors for silent regressions. Works 
with all CC versions including the v2.1.113+ Bun binary.
  README.md:8:
> README.md:9:> **v3.0.3** — Local HTTP proxy with 7 hot-reloadable extensions. A/B tested on v2.1.117: **95.5% cache 
hit rate through proxy vs 82.3% direct** on first warm turn. [Full release notes 
→](https://github.com/cnighswonger/[7mclaude-code-cache-fix[0m/releases/tag/v3.0.0)[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:10:[0m
> README.md:11:> **Opus 4.7 advisory:** Metered data shows 4.7 burns Q5h quota at **~2.4x the rate of 4.6** for 
equivalent visible token counts ([independently confirmed by 
@ArkNill](https://github.com/ArkNill/claude-code-hidden-problem-analysis/blob/main/16_OPUS-47-ADVISORY.md)). Two 
factors: a new tokenizer (up to 35% more tokens, 
[documented](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/whats-new-claude-4-7)) and adaptive thinking 
overhead (~105%, not documented in usage response). The Q5h impact compounds into **Q7d** — the weekly quota ceiling 
that most heavy users will hit first. Workaround: `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADAPTIVE_THINKING=1` reduces burn by ~3.3x but 
may reduce quality on complex tasks. See [Discussion 
#25](https://github.com/cnighswonger/[7mclaude-code-cache-fix[0m/discussions/25) [0m[7m[0m(initial [0m[7m[0mobservation) [0m[7m[0mand [0m[7m[0m[Discussion [0m
[7m[0m#42](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/discussions/42) [0m[7m[0m(controlled [0m[7m[0mA/B [0m[7m[0mdata [0m[7m[0m+ [0m[7m[0mQ7d [0m[7m[0manalysis).[0m

> README.md:68:**Auto-recovery (Linux):** `install-service` also drops a healthcheck companion 
(`cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.service` + `.timer`). The timer fires every 2 minutes; the oneshot service runs `curl 
-fs http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health` and `systemctl --user start cache-fix-proxy.service` if the probe fails. This 
recovers the proxy from any stop — clean or unclean, expected or unexpected — within 2 minutes. Background: 
`[7mRestart[0m=on-failure` [0m[7m[0mdoesn't [0m[7m[0mfire [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0mclean [0m[7m[0mstops, [0m[7m[0mso [0m[7m[0mbefore [0m[7m[0mthis [0m[7m[0mcompanion [0m[7m[0mexisted, [0m[7m[0ma [0m[7m[0m`systemctl [0m[7m[0mstop` [0m[7m[0mfrom [0m[7m[0many [0m[7m[0msource [0m
[7m[0m(including [0m[7m[0munidentified [0m[7m[0mones [0m[7m[0mduring [0m[7m[0man [0m[7m[0mAnthropic [0m[7m[0moutage [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0m2026-04-25) [0m[7m[0mwould [0m[7m[0mleave [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mproxy [0m[7m[0mdown [0m[7m[0mindefinitely. [0m[7m[0mmacOS [0m
[7m[0mdoesn't [0m[7m[0mneed [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mcompanion [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0mlaunchd's [0m[7m[0m`KeepAlive` [0m[7m[0malready [0m[7m[0mauto-restarts [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0many [0m[7m[0mexit.[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:69:[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:70:On macOS:[0m

> README.md:117:**Linux note:** the chained-upstream `[7mhost[0m.docker.internal` [0m[7m[0mexample [0m[7m[0mbelow [0m[7m[0mis [0m[7m[0mautomatic [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0mDocker [0m
[7m[0mDesktop [0m[7m[0m(macOS [0m[7m[0m/ [0m[7m[0mWindows). [0m[7m[0mOn [0m[7m[0mplain [0m[7m[0mLinux [0m[7m[0mDocker [0m[7m[0mEngine [0m[7m[0myou [0m[7m[0musually [0m[7m[0mneed [0m
[7m[0m`--add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway` [0m[7m[0mso [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mname [0m[7m[0mresolves [0m[7m[0mto [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mhost [0m[7m[0mbridge. [0m[7m[0mWithout [0m[7m[0mit, [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mcontainer's [0m
[7m[0mname [0m[7m[0mlookup [0m[7m[0mfails [0m[7m[0mand [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mproxy [0m[7m[0mcan't [0m[7m[0mreach [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mupstream [0m[7m[0mservice [0m[7m[0mrunning [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mhost. [0m[7m[0mExample [0m[7m[0mchaining [0m[7m[0mcache-fix [0m[7m[0mproxy [0m
[7m[0mthrough [0m[7m[0m`llm-relay` [0m[7m[0mrunning [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mhost:[0m

[7m[0m  README.md:134:[0m

[7m[0m  README.md:142:| `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_TIMEOUT` | `600000` | Request timeout in milliseconds |[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:143:| `CACHE_FIX_EXTENSIONS_DIR` | `proxy/extensions/` | Directory for extension `.mjs` files |[0m
> README.md:144:| `CACHE_FIX_EXTENSIONS_[7mCONFIG[0m` | `proxy/extensions.json` | Extension configuration file |[0m

> README.md:147:### Corporate [7menvironment[0ms (proxies, custom CAs)[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:148:[0m
> README.md:149:The proxy honors the following [7menvironment [0m[7m[0mvariables [0m[7m[0mwhen [0m[7m[0mforwarding [0m[7m[0mto [0m[7m[0m`api.anthropic.com`. [0m[7m[0mBehind [0m
[7m[0mZscaler [0m[7m[0m/ [0m[7m[0mNetskope [0m[7m[0m/ [0m[7m[0mForcepoint [0m[7m[0m/ [0m[7m[0mBluecoat [0m[7m[0m/ [0m[7m[0mcorporate [0m[7m[0msquid, [0m[7m[0mset [0m[7m[0mthese [0m[7m[0min [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mproxy's [0m[7m[0menvironment.[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:150:[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:151:| Variable | Effect |[0m
  README.md:152:|----------|--------|
  README.md:153:| `HTTPS_PROXY` / `HTTP_PROXY` (and lowercase variants) | Routes upstream requests through the 
corporate HTTP CONNECT proxy. |
> README.md:154:| `NO_PROXY` | Comma-separated [7mhost[0m list to bypass the proxy. Supports `*` and `.suffix.example.com`. |[0m
[7m [0m[7m [0m[7m[0mREADME.md:155:| [0m[7m[0m`CACHE_FIX_PROXY_CA_FILE` [0m[7m[0m| [0m[7m[0mPath [0m[7m[0mto [0m[7m[0ma [0m[7m[0mPEM [0m[7m[0mfile [0m[7m[0mwith [0m[7m[0mone [0m[7m[0mor [0m[7m[0mmore [0m[7m[0mextra [0m[7m[0mCA [0m[7m[0mcertificates [0m[7m[0m(for [0m
[7m[0mSSL-inspecting [0m[7m[0mproxies). [0m[7m[0m|[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:156:| `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` | Standard Node mechanism — also honored. |[0m
> README.md:157:| `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_REJECT_UN[7mAUTH[0mORIZED=0` [0m[7m[0m| [0m[7m[0m**Insecure [0m[7m[0mescape [0m[7m[0mhatch.** [0m[7m[0mDisables [0m[7m[0mTLS [0m[7m[0mverification. [0m[7m[0mUse [0m
[7m[0monly [0m[7m[0mas [0m[7m[0ma [0m[7m[0mlast [0m[7m[0mresort [0m[7m[0mwhile [0m[7m[0myou [0m[7m[0mwait [0m[7m[0mfor [0m[7m[0mIT [0m[7m[0mto [0m[7m[0mprovide [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mcorp [0m[7m[0mCA [0m[7m[0mbundle. [0m[7m[0m|[0m

  README.md:204:**What it does:** Modifies outgoing request structure (block order, fingerprint, TTL, git-status) to 
fix cache bugs. Reads response headers and SSE usage data for monitoring.
  README.md:205:
> README.md:206:**What it does NOT do:** No [7mnetwork [0m[7m[0mcalls [0m[7m[0mfrom [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mproxy [0m[7m[0mor [0m[7m[0minterceptor. [0m[7m[0mAll [0m[7m[0mtelemetry [0m[7m[0mis [0m[7m[0mwritten [0m[7m[0mto [0m
[7m[0mlocal [0m[7m[0mfiles [0m[7m[0munder [0m[7m[0m`~/.claude/`. [0m[7m[0mNo [0m[7m[0mdata [0m[7m[0mleaves [0m[7m[0myour [0m[7m[0mmachine.[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:207:[0m
[7m [0m[7m [0m[7m[0mREADME.md:208:**Supply [0m[7m[0mchain:** [0m[7m[0mProxy [0m[7m[0mmode: [0m[7m[0m7 [0m[7m[0msmall [0m[7m[0mextension [0m[7m[0mmodules [0m[7m[0min [0m[7m[0m`proxy/extensions/` [0m[7m[0m(each [0m[7m[0munder [0m[7m[0m200 [0m[7m[0mlines). [0m
[7m[0mPreload [0m[7m[0mmode: [0m[7m[0msingle [0m[7m[0munminified [0m[7m[0mfile [0m[7m[0m(`preload.mjs`, [0m[7m[0m~1,700 [0m[7m[0mlines). [0m[7m[0mOne [0m[7m[0mdev [0m[7m[0mdependency [0m[7m[0m(`zod` [0m[7m[0mfor [0m[7m[0mschema [0m[7m[0mvalidation [0m[7m[0min [0m
[7m[0mtests [0m[7m[0monly). [0m[7m[0mReview [0m[7m[0mbefore [0m[7m[0minstalling. [0m[7m[0mnpm [0m[7m[0mprovenance [0m[7m[0mlinks [0m[7m[0meach [0m[7m[0mpublished [0m[7m[0mversion [0m[7m[0mto [0m[7m[0mits [0m[7m[0msource [0m[7m[0mcommit.[0m
  README.md:226:## How it works
  README.md:227:
> README.md:228:**Proxy mode** (v3.0.0+): An HTTP server on `[7mlocalhost[0m:9801` [0m[7m[0mintercepts [0m[7m[0m`POST [0m[7m[0m/v1/messages` [0m[7m[0mrequests. [0m
[7m[0mSeven [0m[7m[0mextension [0m[7m[0mmodules [0m[7m[0mprocess [0m[7m[0meach [0m[7m[0mrequest [0m[7m[0mthrough [0m[7m[0ma [0m[7m[0mpipeline [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0mnormalizing [0m[7m[0mblock [0m[7m[0morder, [0m[7m[0mstripping [0m[7m[0mfingerprints, [0m
[7m[0mstabilizing [0m[7m[0mtool [0m[7m[0msort, [0m[7m[0mmanaging [0m[7m[0mTTL [0m[7m[0mmarkers. [0m[7m[0mExtensions [0m[7m[0mare [0m[7m[0mhot-reloadable [0m[7m[0m`.mjs` [0m[7m[0mfiles [0m[7m[0mconfigured [0m[7m[0min [0m
[7m[0m`proxy/extensions.json`. [0m[7m[0mAll [0m[7m[0mother [0m[7m[0mtraffic [0m[7m[0mpasses [0m[7m[0mthrough [0m[7m[0muntouched.[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:229:[0m
[7m [0m[7m [0m[7m[0mREADME.md:230:**Preload [0m[7m[0mmode** [0m[7m[0m(v2.x): [0m[7m[0mA [0m[7m[0mNode.js [0m[7m[0m`--import` [0m[7m[0mmodule [0m[7m[0mthat [0m[7m[0mpatches [0m[7m[0m`globalThis.fetch` [0m[7m[0mbefore [0m[7m[0mClaude [0m
[7m[0mCode [0m[7m[0mmakes [0m[7m[0mAPI [0m[7m[0mcalls. [0m[7m[0mApplies [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0msame [0m[7m[0mfixes [0m[7m[0minline [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0mscans [0m[7m[0muser [0m[7m[0mmessages [0m[7m[0mfor [0m[7m[0mrelocated [0m[7m[0mblocks, [0m[7m[0msorts [0m[7m[0mtools, [0m
[7m[0mrecomputes [0m[7m[0mfingerprints, [0m[7m[0minjects [0m[7m[0mTTL [0m[7m[0mmarkers.[0m
  README.md:294:```bash
  README.md:295:mkdir -p ~/.claude/hooks
> README.md:296:cp "$(npm root -g)/[7mclaude-code-cache-fix[0m/tools/quota-statusline.sh" ~/.claude/hooks/[0m

> README.md:325:**Why we don't ship a proxy extension for this:** the proxy intercepts requests after Claude Code has 
already [7mcompose[0md [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0msystem [0m[7m[0mprompt [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0mby [0m[7m[0mthen [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mvolatile [0m[7m[0m`git [0m[7m[0mstatus` [0m[7m[0mtext [0m[7m[0mis [0m[7m[0malready [0m[7m[0mpart [0m[7m[0mof [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mprefix [0m[7m[0mthat [0m[7m[0mthe [0m
[7m[0mmodel [0m[7m[0mconditioned [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0min [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mprevious [0m[7m[0mturn, [0m[7m[0mand [0m[7m[0mstripping [0m[7m[0mit [0m[7m[0mpost-hoc [0m[7m[0mwould [0m[7m[0mitself [0m[7m[0mbust [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mcache. [0m[7m[0mThe [0m[7m[0mfix [0m[7m[0mhas [0m[7m[0mto [0m
[7m[0mhappen [0m[7m[0mat [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0msource. [0m[7m[0m`CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_GIT_INSTRUCTIONS=1` [0m[7m[0mprevents [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0minjection [0m[7m[0mbefore [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mprompt [0m[7m[0mis [0m[7m[0mcomposed, [0m
[7m[0mwhich [0m[7m[0mis [0m[7m[0mwhy [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mnative [0m[7m[0mflag [0m[7m[0mis [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mright [0m[7m[0mtool. [0m[7m[0mStripping [0m[7m[0mpost-hoc [0m[7m[0mwould [0m[7m[0malso [0m[7m[0mremove [0m[7m[0mmodel-visible [0m[7m[0mcontext [0m[7m[0mthat [0m[7m[0man [0m
[7m[0mexplicit [0m[7m[0mBash [0m[7m[0mcall [0m[7m[0mcan [0m[7m[0mrecover, [0m[7m[0mand [0m[7m[0mwould [0m[7m[0mrisk [0m[7m[0mfalse-positive [0m[7m[0mmatches [0m[7m[0magainst [0m[7m[0massistant-written [0m[7m[0mtext.[0m

That way it works in both modes (and on [7mhost[0ms [0m[7m[0mmid-upgrade). [0m[7m[0mThe [0m[7m[0msession [0m[7m[0mid [0m[7m[0musually [0m[7m[0mcomes [0m[7m[0mfrom [0m[7m[0mClaude [0m[7m[0mCode's [0m[7m[0mstdin [0m[7m[0mwhen [0m

[7m[0m`~/.claude/projects/*/*.jsonl` [0m[7m[0mfilename.[0m

[7m[0m  README.md:347:**Bash (statusline-style):**[0m
  README.md:437:### Why per-session
  README.md:438:
> README.md:439:On multi-agent [7mhost[0ms [0m[7m[0m(multiple [0m[7m[0mClaude [0m[7m[0mCode [0m[7m[0msessions [0m[7m[0msharing [0m[7m[0mone [0m[7m[0mproxy), [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mpre-v3.5.0 [0m[7m[0msingle [0m[7m[0mglobal [0m
[7m[0mfile [0m[7m[0mcaused [0m[7m[0mevery [0m[7m[0msession [0m[7m[0mto [0m[7m[0moverwrite [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mothers' [0m[7m[0mcache [0m[7m[0mstats [0m[7m[0mwith [0m[7m[0meach [0m[7m[0mresponse. [0m[7m[0mA [0m[7m[0mstatusline [0m[7m[0mreading [0m[7m[0mfrom [0m[7m[0msession [0m[7m[0mA [0m
[7m[0mwould [0m[7m[0mshow [0m[7m[0msession [0m[7m[0mB's [0m[7m[0mTTL [0m[7m[0mtier [0m[7m[0mwhenever [0m[7m[0mB [0m[7m[0msent [0m[7m[0ma [0m[7m[0mrequest [0m[7m[0mmore [0m[7m[0mrecently. [0m[7m[0mPer-session [0m[7m[0mfiles [0m[7m[0mplus [0m[7m[0man [0m[7m[0maccount-global [0m
[7m[0mquota [0m[7m[0mfile [0m[7m[0mresolve [0m[7m[0mthis [0m[7m[0mwithout [0m[7m[0mlosing [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0measy [0m[7m[0maccount-wide [0m[7m[0mview. [0m[7m[0mSee [0m
[7m[0m[#104](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/issues/104) [0m[7m[0mfor [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0moriginal [0m[7m[0mreport.[0m

[7m[0m  README.md:441:## Image stripping (preload mode)[0m
  README.md:466:| **Any single image too large** | `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_MAX_DIM=2000` | Replaces images exceeding the 
dimension limit with a forensic placeholder noting the original dimensions. Covers both user-message direct images and 
tool_result-nested images. |
  README.md:467:
> README.md:468:The two [7mcompose[0m: [0m[7m[0mwith [0m[7m[0mboth [0m[7m[0mset, [0m[7m[0m`KEEP_LAST` [0m[7m[0mruns [0m[7m[0mfirst [0m[7m[0m(drops [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mcount), [0m[7m[0mthen [0m[7m[0m`MAX_DIM` [0m[7m[0mruns [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0mwhat [0m
[7m[0mremains [0m[7m[0m(caps [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0msize [0m[7m[0mof [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mkept [0m[7m[0mones). [0m[7m[0mCommon [0m[7m[0mtriggers [0m[7m[0mfor [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mdimension [0m[7m[0maxis: [0m[7m[0mhi-res [0m[7m[0mmanuscript [0m[7m[0mscans, [0m[7m[0mretina [0m
[7m[0mscreenshots, [0m[7m[0mphotos [0m[7m[0mat [0m[7m[0mfull [0m[7m[0mresolution.[0m

  README.md:585:The preload interceptor includes monitoring for microcompact degradation, false rate limiters, 
GrowthBook flag state, usage telemetry, and cost reporting. Quota tracking works in both proxy and preload modes via 
`~/.claude/quota-status/` (proxy: per-session split) or `~/.claude/quota-status.json` (preload: single-session legacy 
path).
  README.md:586:

[7m[0m  README.md:588:[0m
[7m[0m  README.md:589:## Limitations[0m
  README.md:592:- **Overage TTL downgrade** — Exceeding 100% of the 5-hour quota triggers a server-enforced TTL 
downgrade from 1h to 5m. This is server-side and cannot be fixed client-side. The proxy/interceptor prevents the cache 
instability that can push you into overage in the first place.
  README.md:593:- **Microcompact is not preventable** — The monitoring features detect context degradation but cannot 
prevent it. Microcompact and budget enforcement are server-controlled via GrowthBook flags with no client-side disable 
option.
> README.md:594:- **System prompt rewrite is experimental** — Preload-only, opt-in. Not proven to be the cause of 
behavior differences discussed in community re[7mports[0m. [0m[7m[0mUse [0m[7m[0mat [0m[7m[0myour [0m[7m[0mown [0m[7m[0mrisk.[0m
[7m [0m[7m [0m[7m[0mREADME.md:595:- [0m[7m[0m**Version [0m[7m[0mcoupling** [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0mThe [0m[7m[0mfingerprint [0m[7m[0msalt [0m[7m[0mand [0m[7m[0mblock [0m[7m[0mdetection [0m[7m[0mheuristics [0m[7m[0mare [0m[7m[0mderived [0m[7m[0mfrom [0m[7m[0mClaude [0m
[7m[0mCode [0m[7m[0minternals. [0m[7m[0mA [0m[7m[0mmajor [0m[7m[0mrefactor [0m[7m[0mcould [0m[7m[0mrequire [0m[7m[0man [0m[7m[0mupdate [0m[7m[0mto [0m[7m[0mthis [0m[7m[0mpackage.[0m

  CHANGELOG.md:17:### Security
  CHANGELOG.md:18:
> CHANGELOG.md:19:- **`tools/quota-statusline.sh`: shell injection via Python triple-quoted literal (#108).** The 
v3.5.0 statusline rewrite interpolated CC's hook stdin payload directly into a Python triple-quoted string 
(`json.loads('''$input''')`). A `'''` byte sequence anywhere in the payload closed the literal early and let the 
following bytes execute as Python in the user's CC process. Because CC's hook payload reflects user-controlled paths 
(`cwd`, `workspace.current_dir`, `workspace.project_dir`, `transcript_path`) and apostrophes are legal in filesystem 
paths, a [7mhost[0mile [0m[7m[0mdirectory [0m[7m[0mname [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0mdisk [0m[7m[0m(planted [0m[7m[0mvia [0m[7m[0m`git [0m[7m[0mclone`, [0m[7m[0marchive [0m[7m[0mextraction, [0m[7m[0mnpm [0m[7m[0mpackage, [0m[7m[0metc.) [0m[7m[0mcould [0m[7m[0mtrigger [0m
[7m[0marbitrary [0m[7m[0mlocal [0m[7m[0mcode [0m[7m[0mexecution [0m[7m[0mat [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0muser's [0m[7m[0mprivilege [0m[7m[0mevery [0m[7m[0mtime [0m[7m[0mCC [0m[7m[0mredrew [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mstatusline. [0m[7m[0m**Severity: [0m[7m[0mlocal [0m[7m[0mcode [0m
[7m[0mexecution, [0m[7m[0mpersistent [0m[7m[0mre-fire [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0mevery [0m[7m[0mstatusline [0m[7m[0mtick, [0m[7m[0mno [0m[7m[0muser [0m[7m[0minteraction [0m[7m[0mbeyond [0m[7m[0m`cd`-ing [0m[7m[0minto [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mhostile [0m[7m[0mpath.** [0m
[7m[0mFix: [0m[7m[0mcapture [0m[7m[0mstdin [0m[7m[0min [0m[7m[0mbash, [0m[7m[0m`export [0m[7m[0mCC_INPUT`, [0m[7m[0mand [0m[7m[0mpipe [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mPython [0m[7m[0msource [0m[7m[0mthrough [0m[7m[0ma [0m[7m[0msingle-quoted [0m[7m[0mheredoc [0m
[7m[0m(`<<'PYEOF'`) [0m[7m[0mwhich [0m[7m[0mdisables [0m[7m[0mALL [0m[7m[0mbash [0m[7m[0minterpolation [0m[7m[0min [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mbody. [0m[7m[0mPython [0m[7m[0mnow [0m[7m[0mreads [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mJSON [0m[7m[0mvia [0m
[7m[0m`os.environ.get('CC_INPUT')`, [0m[7m[0mwhere [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mbytes [0m[7m[0mare [0m[7m[0minert [0m[7m[0mat [0m[7m[0mevery [0m[7m[0mlayer. [0m[7m[0mAdds [0m[7m[0mT6 [0m[7m[0m+ [0m[7m[0mT7 [0m[7m[0mregression [0m[7m[0mtests [0m[7m[0mthat [0m[7m[0mdrive [0m[7m[0mthe [0m
[7m[0mexact [0m[7m[0m`'''+__import__('os').system(...)+'''` [0m[7m[0mpattern [0m[7m[0magainst [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mscript [0m[7m[0munder [0m[7m[0ma [0m[7m[0mtmpdir-rooted [0m[7m[0m`HOME` [0m[7m[0mand [0m[7m[0massert [0m[7m[0mthe [0m
[7m[0msentinel [0m[7m[0mfile [0m[7m[0mis [0m[7m[0mnever [0m[7m[0mcreated. [0m[7m[0mReported [0m[7m[0mby [0m[7m[0m[@schuay [0m[7m[0m(Jakob [0m[7m[0mLinke)](https://github.com/schuay) [0m[7m[0min [0m
[7m[0m[#108](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/issues/108) [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0mthank [0m[7m[0myou [0m[7m[0mfor [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mresponsible [0m[7m[0mdisclosure.[0m

  CHANGELOG.md:28:
> CHANGELOG.md:29:- `cache-telemetry`: session-id headers (`x-claude-code-session-id` and the legacy fallbacks) live 
on the **request**, not the response. The v3.5.0 implementation read them from `ctx.headers` in `onResponseStart` — 
but that ctx carries response headers, and Anthropic doesn't echo session-id back. Net effect on multi-agent [7mhost[0ms [0m
[7m[0mrunning [0m[7m[0mv3.5.0: [0m[7m[0mevery [0m[7m[0mper-session [0m[7m[0mfile [0m[7m[0mlanded [0m[7m[0mat [0m[7m[0m`sessions/unknown.json` [0m[7m[0mwith [0m[7m[0m`session_id: [0m[7m[0mnull`, [0m[7m[0mdefeating [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mwhole [0m
[7m[0mpoint [0m[7m[0mof [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mper-session [0m[7m[0msplit. [0m[7m[0mCaptured [0m[7m[0mproduction [0m[7m[0mfailure [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0mvisits-01 [0m[7m[0mimmediately [0m[7m[0mafter [0m[7m[0mv3.5.0 [0m[7m[0mrollout. [0m[7m[0mFix [0m[7m[0mmoves [0m
[7m[0msession-id [0m[7m[0mresolution [0m[7m[0minto [0m[7m[0ma [0m[7m[0mnew [0m[7m[0m`onRequest` [0m[7m[0mhook [0m[7m[0m(request [0m[7m[0mheaders); [0m[7m[0m`onStreamEvent` [0m[7m[0mreads [0m[7m[0mfrom [0m[7m[0m`ctx.meta._sessionId` [0m
[7m[0mas [0m[7m[0mbefore. [0m[7m[0mThe [0m[7m[0mproxy [0m[7m[0mserver [0m[7m[0mpasses [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0msame [0m[7m[0m`meta` [0m[7m[0mobject [0m[7m[0mthrough [0m[7m[0m`onRequest [0m[7m[0m→ [0m[7m[0monResponseStart [0m[7m[0m→ [0m[7m[0monStreamEvent`, [0m[7m[0mso [0m

coverage. (#99, closes #96; @vmfarms surfaced this)

  CHANGELOG.md:117:New `CACHE_FIX_IMAGE_MAX_DIM=<pixels>` env var on the existing `image-strip` extension. When an 
image's pixel dimensions exceed the cap (Anthropic's per-image dimension ceiling for many-image requests is 2000px), 
the image is replaced with a forensic placeholder noting the original dimensions and tool_use_id. Covers both 
user-message direct images and tool_result-nested images. Pure-JS PNG and JPEG header parsing in new 
`proxy/image-dimensions.mjs` — no native dependencies.
  CHANGELOG.md:118:

[7m[0m  CHANGELOG.md:139:**`usage-log` rewritten to MeterRowSchema v:1** (#81, closes #70):[0m
  CHANGELOG.md:141:The proxy's `~/.claude/usage.jsonl` now emits exactly the 29-field record shape that 
`claude-code-meter`'s strict `z.strictObject({ v: z.literal(1), ... })` validator expects. The wire format is now the 
cross-repo contract — claude-meter v0.4.0+ tails the proxy's JSONL via `claude-meter ingest --watch`, validates 
strictly, and persists into the local store the existing analyze/share/status/history/rates already read from. 
**Breaking change for the `usage-log` row format** — old 9-field rows (with `peak_hour`) in any pre-existing 
`usage.jsonl` files will fail claude-meter's strict validator and be skipped on the reader side. `peak_hour` is no 
longer in the wire format (recomputable from `ts` if needed). `org_id` hashed with `sha256(raw).digest("hex").slice(0, 
16)` — bit-exact match with claude-meter's algorithm, never raw. Activation pattern unchanged: opt-in via 
`extensions.json` entry, `CACHE_FIX_USAGE_LOG` is path override only.
  CHANGELOG.md:142:
> CHANGELOG.md:143:**Cross-repo release ordering**: cache-fix v3.2.0 ships first. claude-meter v0.4.0 follows, 
declaring `[7mclaude-code-cache-fix [0m[7m[0m>= [0m[7m[0m3.2.0` [0m[7m[0mas [0m[7m[0mits [0m[7m[0msupported [0m[7m[0mproducer. [0m[7m[0mThe [0m[7m[0mtwo [0m[7m[0mpackages [0m[7m[0mare [0m[7m[0mNOT [0m[7m[0mindependently [0m[7m[0mshippable [0m
[7m[0mfor [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mproxy-mode [0m[7m[0mingestion [0m[7m[0mpath.[0m

  CHANGELOG.md:186:- **`prefix-diff`** (opt-in via `CACHE_FIX_PREFIXDIFF=1`) — pure diagnostic. On every request, 
snapshots a small projection of the prefix (system prompt + tools + first 5 messages) to 
`~/.claude/cache-fix-snapshots/<key>-last.json`. If a prior snapshot exists and content differs, also writes 
`<key>-diff.json` and emits a one-line stderr summary. Atomic writes; per-call diff (no boot-flag gating). Closes #59 
item 9. (#65)
> CHANGELOG.md:187:- **`deferred-tools-restore`** (defaults ON; opt out via `CACHE_FIX_SKIP_DEFERRED_TOOLS_RESTORE=1`) 
— preserves cache prefix across the MCP-reconnect race. On `claude --continue`, if MCP servers haven't reconnected 
before the first post-resume request, the deferred-tools attachment block at `msg[0]` shrinks dramatically and busts 
the cache at the very top (entire ~940K prompt re-caches). This extension persists the clean form of the block and 
substitutes it on subsequent shrunken requests, with strict downgrade guard (snapshot must be strictly longer than 
current). Snapshot keyed on the cwd parsed from CC's `# [7mEnvironment[0m` [0m[7m[0msection [0m[7m[0min [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0msystem [0m[7m[0mprompt [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0mline-based [0m[7m[0msection [0m
[7m[0mparser [0m[7m[0mwith [0m[7m[0mambiguity [0m[7m[0mguard [0m[7m[0mfails [0m[7m[0mopen [0m[7m[0mon [0m[7m[0mparse [0m[7m[0mfailure. [0m[7m[0mCloses [0m[7m[0m#59 [0m[7m[0mitem [0m[7m[0m6. [0m[7m[0m(#66)[0m
[7m[0m  CHANGELOG.md:188:[0m
> CHANGELOG.md:189:**Default [7mconfig[0m update** (#69):[0m
[7m[0m  CHANGELOG.md:190:[0m

  CHANGELOG.md:279:### Opus 4.7 advisory
  CHANGELOG.md:280:
> CHANGELOG.md:281:Metered data shows Opus 4.7 burns Q5h at ~2.4x the rate of 4.6 due to invisible adaptive thinking 
tokens not reported in the API usage response. Workaround: `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADAPTIVE_THINKING=1` (may reduce 
quality). See [Discussion #25](https://github.com/cnighswonger/[7mclaude-code-cache-fix[0m/discussions/25).[0m

`<local-command-stdout>` blocks that bleed into `messages[0]` after `/clear`, breaking prefix cache match vs a fresh 
session. Credit: [@wadabum](https://github.com/wadabum) (anthropics/claude-code#47756).

`claude-code-meter` installed by reading quota data from the interceptor's `quota-status.json`. Fixes #18. Credit: 
[@dmurat](https://github.com/dmurat).

> CHANGELOG.md:317:- **[7mConfig[0murable [0m[7m[0mTTL [0m[7m[0mper [0m[7m[0mrequest [0m[7m[0mtype** [0m[7m[0m(#14) [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0mTTL [0m[7m[0minjection [0m[7m[0mnow [0m[7m[0mdistinguishes [0m[7m[0mmain-thread [0m[7m[0mfrom [0m

[7m[0m`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` [0m[7m[0mcan [0m[7m[0mnow [0m[7m[0mcontrol [0m[7m[0mTTL [0m[7m[0mper [0m[7m[0mcall [0m[7m[0mtype.[0m
[7m [0m[7m [0m[7m[0mCHANGELOG.md:318:- [0m[7m[0m**Cache [0m[7m[0mbreakpoint [0m[7m[0mdump** [0m[7m[0m(#12) [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0mDiagnostic [0m[7m[0menv [0m[7m[0mvar [0m[7m[0m`CACHE_FIX_DUMP_BREAKPOINTS=<path>` [0m[7m[0mwrites [0m

[7m[0midentified [0m[7m[0mby [0m[7m[0m@wadabum.[0m
[7m [0m[7m [0m[7m[0mCHANGELOG.md:319:- [0m[7m[0m**Cost-report [0m[7m[0mtier [0m[7m[0mfix** [0m[7m[0m(#7) [0m[7m[0m— [0m[7m[0m`cost-report.mjs` [0m[7m[0mnow [0m[7m[0mcorrectly [0m[7m[0massigns [0m[7m[0mcache [0m[7m[0mcreation [0m[7m[0mtokens [0m[7m[0mto [0m
[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0m1h [0m[7m[0mwrite [0m[7m[0mrate [0m[7m[0mwhen [0m[7m[0m`ephemeral_1h_input_tokens [0m[7m[0m> [0m[7m[0m0`. [0m[7m[0mPreviously [0m[7m[0mall [0m[7m[0mcreation [0m[7m[0mwas [0m[7m[0massumed [0m[7m[0m5m [0m[7m[0mwhen [0m[7m[0mthe [0m[7m[0mephemeral [0m
[7m[0mbreakdown [0m[7m[0mfields [0m[7m[0mwere [0m[7m[0mzero, [0m[7m[0munderstating [0m[7m[0mcost [0m[7m[0mfor [0m[7m[0m1h-tier [0m[7m[0msessions.[0m
  CHANGELOG.md:353:- **Full `anthropic-*` response header capture** — Widened the response header capture in 
`preload.mjs` from specific unified-ratelimit headers to the entire `anthropic-*` namespace plus 
`request-id`/`cf-ray`. Saved to `~/.claude/quota-status.json` under a new `all_headers` key. Future-proofs against 
Anthropic adding new headers without requiring code changes. Pattern borrowed from @fgrosswig's claude-usage-dashboard 
proxy.
  CHANGELOG.md:354:- **`cost-factor` metric in `cost-report.mjs`** — Adds an overhead-ratio metric: `(input + output + 
cache_read + cache_creation) / output`. Single-number indicator of how much context is being paid per useful output 
token; rising values over long sessions signal cache-efficiency degradation. Surfaced in text, JSON, and Markdown 
output modes. Credit: @fgrosswig (methodology from claude-usage-dashboard).
> CHANGELOG.md:355:- **`tools/sim-cost-reconcile.sh`** — One-liner wrapper around `cost-report.mjs` for running 
simulation logs against the Anthropic admin API. Auto-loads the admin key from `~/.[7mconfig[0m/anthropic/admin-key` [0m[7m[0mor [0m

> CHANGELOG.md:385:- **Prompt size measurement** — When `CACHE_FIX_DEBUG=1`, every API call now logs character counts 
for the system prompt, tool schemas, and per-type injected blocks (skills listing, MCP instructions, deferred tools, 
hooks). Helps users with large plugin/skill setups quantify the per-turn token cost of their [7mconfig[0muration.[0m

README.md:7:Cache optimization proxy for [Claude Code](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code). Fixes prompt cache bugs that cause excessive quota burn, stabilizes the request prefix, and monitors for silent regressions. Works with all CC versions including the v2.1.113+ Bun binary.
README.md:15:The proxy works with any CC version — Node.js or Bun binary. It sits between Claude Code and the Anthropic API, applying cache fixes as hot-reloadable extensions.
README.md:36:| `fingerprint-strip` | Removes unstable cc_version fingerprint from system prompt |
README.md:64:systemctl --user enable --now cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.timer   # auto-recovery — see below
README.md:68:**Auto-recovery (Linux):** `install-service` also drops a healthcheck companion (`cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.service` + `.timer`). The timer fires every 2 minutes; the oneshot service runs `curl -fs http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health` and `systemctl --user start cache-fix-proxy.service` if the probe fails. This recovers the proxy from any stop — clean or unclean, expected or unexpected — within 2 minutes. Background: `Restart=on-failure` doesn't fire on clean stops, so before this companion existed, a `systemctl stop` from any source (including unidentified ones during an Anthropic outage on 2026-04-25) would leave the proxy down indefinitely. macOS doesn't need the companion — launchd's `KeepAlive` already auto-restarts on any exit.
README.md:78:The installed config picks up `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT`, `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM`, and `CACHE_FIX_DEBUG` from the env at install time. Re-run `install-service --force` to regenerate after env changes, or edit the service file directly. Pair with `cache-fix-proxy uninstall-service` to remove cleanly (stops, disables, deletes).
README.md:89:### Docker
README.md:94:docker run -d --name cache-fix-proxy \
README.md:97:  ghcr.io/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix:latest
README.md:103:Use `--restart=always` instead of the systemd healthcheck companion — Docker handles auto-recovery natively. Mount nothing; the container is stateless. Override the default port with `-e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT=...`. Override the upstream (e.g. to chain through llm-relay) with `-e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM=http://host.docker.internal:8080`. The image runs as the unprivileged `node` user (uid 1000) and exposes a `HEALTHCHECK` Docker can use for liveness.
README.md:108:docker run -d --name cache-fix-proxy --restart=always -p 9801:9801 \
README.md:112:  ghcr.io/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix:latest
README.md:117:**Linux note:** the chained-upstream `host.docker.internal` example below is automatic on Docker Desktop (macOS / Windows). On plain Linux Docker Engine you usually need `--add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway` so the name resolves to the host bridge. Without it, the container's name lookup fails and the proxy can't reach the upstream service running on the host. Example chaining cache-fix proxy through `llm-relay` running on the host:
README.md:120:docker run -d --name cache-fix-proxy --restart=always -p 9801:9801 \
README.md:121:  --add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway \
README.md:122:  -e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM=http://host.docker.internal:8080 \

README.md:172:If you're on a Node.js-based CC version (v2.1.112 or earlier), the preload interceptor works without a proxy:
README.md:208:**Supply chain:** Proxy mode: 7 small extension modules in `proxy/extensions/` (each under 200 lines). Preload mode: single unminified file (`preload.mjs`, ~1,700 lines). One dev dependency (`zod` for schema validation in tests only). Review before installing. npm provenance links each published version to its source commit.
README.md:220:2. **Fingerprint instability** — The `cc_version` fingerprint (e.g. `2.1.92.a3f`) is computed from `messages[0]` content including meta/attachment blocks. When those blocks shift, the fingerprint changes, the system prompt changes, and cache busts.
README.md:269:Before rewriting the `cc_version` fingerprint, the interceptor verifies that its hardcoded salt and character indices reproduce the fingerprint Claude Code sent. If verification fails (CC changed its algorithm), the rewrite is skipped automatically. This ensures the interceptor can never make cache performance *worse* than stock CC.
README.md:435:The shipped [`tools/quota-statusline.sh`](tools/quota-statusline.sh) is the reference implementation for the bash version. The [`/coffee` skill](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-coffee) v1.4.0 is the reference for the per-session warmth gate.
README.md:595:- **Version coupling** — The fingerprint salt and block detection heuristics are derived from Claude Code internals. A major refactor could require an update to this package.

CHANGELOG.md:5:## [3.5.3] - 2026-05-08
CHANGELOG.md:155:- Refuses to overwrite existing config without `--force`. Picks up `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT`, `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM`, `CACHE_FIX_DEBUG` from the env at install time.
CHANGELOG.md:158:**Healthcheck companion for proxy auto-recovery** (#75):
CHANGELOG.md:160:After the 2026-04-25 incident where the proxy was stopped by an unidentified caller during the Anthropic outage and stayed down for ~10 hours (`Restart=on-failure` doesn't fire on clean stops), `install-service` now also drops a healthcheck companion on Linux:
CHANGELOG.md:162:- `cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.service` — oneshot that does `curl -fs http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health` and `systemctl --user start cache-fix-proxy.service` if the probe fails
CHANGELOG.md:163:- `cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.timer` — fires the oneshot 30s after boot then every 2 minutes (AccuracySec=15s)
CHANGELOG.md:170:- Port string is now validated before being interpolated into the healthcheck shell command. A hostile `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT` value (with shell metacharacters) would have allowed command injection; rejected with a clear error message now.
CHANGELOG.md:171:- Symmetric existence check on the healthcheck pair: refuses overwrite if either the service file OR the timer file exists (caught case where one was a half-installed stale artifact).
CHANGELOG.md:172:- Half-install rollback: if the healthcheck install throws after the main unit is written, the main unit is removed so users aren't left in a partial state.
CHANGELOG.md:226:- **BUGFIX: `manual-compact.sh` path conversion failed on directories with underscores** — CC normalizes underscores to hyphens in project paths (e.g. `kanfei_test` → `kanfei-test`). The script now handles this. Also improved output to show the exact copy-paste message with the real session ID.
CHANGELOG.md:289:- **Fingerprint verification fix for CC v2.1.108+** — CC v2.1.108 changed fingerprint computation to skip `<system-reminder>` blocks via an `isMeta` filter. The safety check now tries both the new extraction method (v2.1.108+) and the legacy method, keeping fingerprint stabilization working across CC versions. `CACHE_FIX_SKIP_FINGERPRINT=1` workaround is no longer needed. Credit: [@ArkNill](https://github.com/ArkNill) (PR #21).
CHANGELOG.md:323:- **nvm-compatible wrapper script** — README wrapper now uses `npm root -g` for dynamic path resolution instead of hardcoded `$HOME/.npm-global`. Fixes setup for nvm, volta, and other Node version managers. Adds existence check for the interceptor module. Credit: [@arjansingh](https://github.com/arjansingh) (PR #15).
CHANGELOG.md:329:- **Fingerprint round-trip safety check (P0)** — Before rewriting `cc_version`, verifies our salt/indices reproduce the fingerprint CC sent. If verification fails (CC changed its algorithm), the rewrite is skipped automatically. The interceptor can never make cache performance *worse* than stock CC.
CHANGELOG.md:344:- **Windows support** — Added `claude-fixed.bat` wrapper for Windows users where `NODE_OPTIONS="--import ..."` doesn't work. Dynamically resolves npm global root, constructs `file:///` URL with forward-slash conversion, launches Claude Code with the interceptor active. Credit: [@TomTheMenace](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/38335).
CHANGELOG.md:350:Investigation release — cross-version regression analysis, interop with @fgrosswig's claude-usage-dashboard, and diagnostic tooling for per-version tool-schema drift.
CHANGELOG.md:352:- **`CACHE_FIX_DUMP_TOOLS` diagnostic hook** — Env-gated dump of the outgoing `tools` array to a JSON file, recording per-tool name, description, schema size, and total serialized size. Used during the 2026-04-11 cross-version regression investigation to identify that Claude Code v2.1.101's +7,207 character tool-schema growth is 92% attributable to two new tools (`Monitor` and `ScheduleWakeup`) shipped in that release. Inert unless `CACHE_FIX_DUMP_TOOLS=<path>` is set.
CHANGELOG.md:358:- **docs/march-23-regression-investigation.md** — Full methodology and measurements from the 2026-04-11 cross-version analysis of Claude Code v2.1.81, v2.1.83, v2.1.90, and v2.1.101. Documents the release-timing argument (regression starts mid-release-cycle → server-side change), per-version prefix sizes, per-section breakdown, per-tool drift table, and the `ScheduleWakeup` tool description quote confirming the 5-minute TTL baseline from Anthropic's own product code.
CHANGELOG.md:362:The CHANGELOG was not kept in sync during this release window. The major shipped features across these versions:
CHANGELOG.md:413:- **Fingerprint instability** — Stabilizes the `cc_version` fingerprint by computing it from real user text instead of meta/attachment blocks

 succeeded in 392ms:
   1: # claude-code-cache-fix proxy — minimal container.
   2: #
   3: # Runs the proxy server on port 9801 (override with -e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT=...).
   4: # Forwards to api.anthropic.com by default (override with -e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM=...).
   5: #
   6: # Usage:
   7: #   docker run -d -p 9801:9801 --name cache-fix-proxy \
   8: #     ghcr.io/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix:latest
   9: #
  10: # Then set ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9801 in the shell that runs claude.
  11: 
  12: FROM node:22-alpine
  13: 
  14: # curl is needed for the HEALTHCHECK directive below (Alpine doesn't ship it).
  15: RUN apk add --no-cache curl
  16: 
  17: WORKDIR /app
  18: 
  19: # Copy package manifests first so npm install is cached separately from source.
  20: COPY package.json package-lock.json* ./
  21: 
  22: # We only need the runtime dependency (hpagent) for the proxy. --omit=dev keeps
  23: # the image small. --no-audit / --no-fund silences npm noise. --ignore-scripts
  24: # skips the postinstall.js wrapper-install that's irrelevant in a container.
  25: RUN npm install --omit=dev --no-audit --no-fund --ignore-scripts
  26: 
  27: # Copy only the files the proxy needs at runtime.
  28: COPY proxy/ ./proxy/
  29: 
  30: # Run as a non-root user. node:22-alpine ships a `node` user by uid 1000.
  31: RUN chown -R node:node /app
  32: USER node
  33: 
  34: # Default port; override with `-e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT=...`.
  35: ENV CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT=9801
  36: 
  37: # Bind to all interfaces by default in container land — the publishing layer
  38: # (-p 9801:9801) is what gates external exposure. The default 127.0.0.1 bind
  39: # would make the proxy unreachable from the host through -p.
  40: ENV CACHE_FIX_PROXY_BIND=0.0.0.0
  41: 
  42: EXPOSE 9801
  43: 
  44: # HEALTHCHECK runs every 30s; the proxy's /health endpoint returns ok JSON
  45: # when the server is listening and extension pipeline is loaded.
  46: HEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=3s --start-period=5s --retries=3 \
  47:   CMD curl -fs "http://127.0.0.1:${CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT}/health" || exit 1
  48: 
  49: CMD ["node", "proxy/server.mjs"]
   1: # claude-code-cache-fix
   2: 
   3: [![npm](https://img.shields.io/npm/v/claude-code-cache-fix?color=blue)](https://www.npmjs.com/package/claude-code-cache-fix) [![Node.js](https://img.shields.io/badge/Node.js-18%2B-green)](https://nodejs.org/) [![License: MIT](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-MIT-yellow)](https://opensource.org/licenses/MIT) [![GitHub stars](https://img.shields.io/github/stars/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix)](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/stargazers)
   4: 
   5: English | [中文](./README.zh.md) | [한국어](./README.ko.md) | [Português](./docs/guia-pt-br.md)
   6: 
   7: Cache optimization proxy for [Claude Code](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code). Fixes prompt cache bugs that cause excessive quota burn, stabilizes the request prefix, and monitors for silent regressions. Works with all CC versions including the v2.1.113+ Bun binary.
   8: 
   9: > **v3.0.3** — Local HTTP proxy with 7 hot-reloadable extensions. A/B tested on v2.1.117: **95.5% cache hit rate through proxy vs 82.3% direct** on first warm turn. [Full release notes →](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/releases/tag/v3.0.0)
  10: 
  11: > **Opus 4.7 advisory:** Metered data shows 4.7 burns Q5h quota at **~2.4x the rate of 4.6** for equivalent visible token counts ([independently confirmed by @ArkNill](https://github.com/ArkNill/claude-code-hidden-problem-analysis/blob/main/16_OPUS-47-ADVISORY.md)). Two factors: a new tokenizer (up to 35% more tokens, [documented](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/whats-new-claude-4-7)) and adaptive thinking overhead (~105%, not documented in usage response). The Q5h impact compounds into **Q7d** — the weekly quota ceiling that most heavy users will hit first. Workaround: `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADAPTIVE_THINKING=1` reduces burn by ~3.3x but may reduce quality on complex tasks. See [Discussion #25](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/discussions/25) (initial observation) and [Discussion #42](https://github.com/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix/discussions/42) (controlled A/B data + Q7d analysis).
  12: 
  13: ## Quick Start: Proxy (recommended)
  14: 
  15: The proxy works with any CC version — Node.js or Bun binary. It sits between Claude Code and the Anthropic API, applying cache fixes as hot-reloadable extensions.
  16: 
  17: ```bash
  18: # Install
  19: npm install -g claude-code-cache-fix
  20: 
  21: # Start the proxy (runs on localhost:9801)
  22: node "$(npm root -g)/claude-code-cache-fix/proxy/server.mjs" &
  23: 
  24: # Launch Claude Code through it
  25: ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9801 claude
  26: ```
  27: 
  28: That's it. The proxy applies all 7 cache-fix extensions automatically. No wrapper scripts, no `NODE_OPTIONS`, no preload.
  29: 
  30: ### What the proxy does
  31: 
  32: On every `/v1/messages` request, 7 extensions run in order:
  33: 
  34: | Extension | What it fixes |
  35: |-----------|--------------|
  36: | `fingerprint-strip` | Removes unstable cc_version fingerprint from system prompt |
  37: | `sort-stabilization` | Deterministic ordering of tool and MCP definitions |
  38: | `ttl-management` | Detects server TTL tier, injects correct cache_control markers |
  39: | `identity-normalization` | Normalizes message identity fields for prefix stability |
  40: | `fresh-session-sort` | Fixes non-deterministic ordering on first turn |
  41: | `cache-control-normalize` | Normalizes cache_control markers across messages |
  42: | `cache-telemetry` | Extracts cache stats from response headers → `~/.claude/quota-status/{account.json,sessions/<id>.json}` |
  43: 
  44: Extensions are hot-reloadable — add, remove, or modify `.mjs` files in `proxy/extensions/` and changes apply to the next request without restarting. Configuration in `proxy/extensions.json`.
  45: 
  46: ### Running as a service
  47: 
  48: **Recommended (Linux/macOS) — `install-service` subcommand:**
  49: 
  50: ```bash
  51: cache-fix-proxy install-service
  52: ```
  53: 
  54: Detects your platform and writes the appropriate config:
  55: 
  56: - **Linux** → `~/.config/systemd/user/cache-fix-proxy.service` (systemd user unit)
  57: - **macOS** → `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.cnighswonger.cache-fix-proxy.plist` (launchd agent)
  58: 
  59: The output prints the next-step commands to enable and start the service. On Linux:
  60: 
  61: ```bash
  62: systemctl --user daemon-reload
  63: systemctl --user enable --now cache-fix-proxy
  64: systemctl --user enable --now cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.timer   # auto-recovery — see below
  65: sudo loginctl enable-linger $USER   # optional: start on boot, not just on login
  66: ```
  67: 
  68: **Auto-recovery (Linux):** `install-service` also drops a healthcheck companion (`cache-fix-proxy-healthcheck.service` + `.timer`). The timer fires every 2 minutes; the oneshot service runs `curl -fs http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health` and `systemctl --user start cache-fix-proxy.service` if the probe fails. This recovers the proxy from any stop — clean or unclean, expected or unexpected — within 2 minutes. Background: `Restart=on-failure` doesn't fire on clean stops, so before this companion existed, a `systemctl stop` from any source (including unidentified ones during an Anthropic outage on 2026-04-25) would leave the proxy down indefinitely. macOS doesn't need the companion — launchd's `KeepAlive` already auto-restarts on any exit.
  69: 
  70: On macOS:
  71: 
  72: ```bash
  73: launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.cnighswonger.cache-fix-proxy.plist
  74: launchctl enable gui/$(id -u)/com.cnighswonger.cache-fix-proxy
  75: launchctl kickstart gui/$(id -u)/com.cnighswonger.cache-fix-proxy
  76: ```
  77: 
  78: The installed config picks up `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT`, `CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM`, and `CACHE_FIX_DEBUG` from the env at install time. Re-run `install-service --force` to regenerate after env changes, or edit the service file directly. Pair with `cache-fix-proxy uninstall-service` to remove cleanly (stops, disables, deletes).
  79: 
  80: The service runs `cache-fix-proxy server` in the foreground, which is just the proxy without the wrapper-mode claude launcher.
  81: 
  82: **Manual (any platform):**
  83: 
  84: ```bash
  85: nohup cache-fix-proxy server > /tmp/cache-fix-proxy.log 2>&1 &
  86: echo 'export ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9801' >> ~/.bashrc
  87: ```
  88: 
  89: ### Docker
  90: 
  91: A multi-arch (amd64, arm64) container image is published to GitHub Container Registry on every release tag.
  92: 
  93: ```bash
  94: docker run -d --name cache-fix-proxy \
  95:   --restart=always \
  96:   -p 9801:9801 \
  97:   ghcr.io/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix:latest
  98: 
  99: # Then in your shell:
 100: export ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9801
 101: ```
 102: 
 103: Use `--restart=always` instead of the systemd healthcheck companion — Docker handles auto-recovery natively. Mount nothing; the container is stateless. Override the default port with `-e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_PORT=...`. Override the upstream (e.g. to chain through llm-relay) with `-e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM=http://host.docker.internal:8080`. The image runs as the unprivileged `node` user (uid 1000) and exposes a `HEALTHCHECK` Docker can use for liveness.
 104: 
 105: For corporate environments behind an SSL-inspecting proxy, mount your CA bundle and set the env vars:
 106: 
 107: ```bash
 108: docker run -d --name cache-fix-proxy --restart=always -p 9801:9801 \
 109:   -e HTTPS_PROXY=http://proxy.corp.example:8080 \
 110:   -e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_CA_FILE=/etc/ssl/corp-ca.pem \
 111:   -v /path/to/zscaler-root.pem:/etc/ssl/corp-ca.pem:ro \
 112:   ghcr.io/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix:latest
 113: ```
 114: 
 115: Image tags: `latest`, `3`, `3.2`, `3.2.1` (semver-ladder, so `3` always points to the newest 3.x). `latest` always tracks the newest tagged release.
 116: 
 117: **Linux note:** the chained-upstream `host.docker.internal` example below is automatic on Docker Desktop (macOS / Windows). On plain Linux Docker Engine you usually need `--add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway` so the name resolves to the host bridge. Without it, the container's name lookup fails and the proxy can't reach the upstream service running on the host. Example chaining cache-fix proxy through `llm-relay` running on the host:
 118: 
 119: ```bash
 120: docker run -d --name cache-fix-proxy --restart=always -p 9801:9801 \
 121:   --add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway \
 122:   -e CACHE_FIX_PROXY_UPSTREAM=http://host.docker.internal:8080 \
 123:   ghcr.io/cnighswonger/claude-code-cache-fix:latest
 124: ```
